### Monitor 62: Inter-Frame Interval by Window

This page's own render loop governed by the compass law: the frame time against its line, native (no governor) beside Omni-Compass.

- **Evidence and state.** Class S; live.
- **Feed.** this page's own clock (no exporter: the page measures itself).
- **Wired.** This page's `drawSelf` → the Python `omnicompass/compass_law.py` → the C++ `cpp/tools/engine_runs.cpp`; held equal by `docs/engine/check_live_engine.py`.
- **Moves.** Every frame: this page's own frame time under the compass law.
- **Knobs.** Frame Load: +0, +8 or +16 ms burned inside every frame of this page; Kill Switch: Omni-Compass ON (green) or engaged (red): every setting handed back to native at once; every Omni-Compass side follows native (white).
- **How to read it.** This page's own render loop governed by the compass law: the time between frames against its line, native (no governor) beside Omni-Compass.
- **What to do with it.** Load the page with the frame-load knob and watch the governor shed chart frames to keep every frame inside its line.

## The page's Appendixes

## A Title Page and Build

**The page, in its own words.**

A live monitor of the Omni-Compass engine and of every benchmark GitHub has run on it: each run played back from its own SHA-256-checked files, each model run by the repository's own code, so the engine can be watched moving instead of only read. Two sides everywhere, and only two: Native, the whole system running by itself, and Omni-Compass on native, the same system with Omni-Compass on top of all of it. On this page Omni-Compass is never run by itself: it always sits on top of a native controller. Beside this title stand the owner's two pictures, each with the engine's live marks over it. The engine that stands is omni-v25 (`docs/OMNI_V25.md`); its road back to omni-v18 is in the disclosures (appendix G), and each earlier engine has its own page (docs/OMNI_V<n>.md). The engine that stands still carries the code omni-v18 and omni-v19 added to run Omni-Compass by itself (its own orchestrator and a pod muscle of its own); it is an opt-in start flag outside the factory tune, and this page shows none of it. Its only modelled record read LOSS: the record of 24 October carried that third arm, Omni-Compass by itself, and it read LOSS in all three of our runs on work and on muscles over the line in eight organisms, 32 rows (on omni-v19, digest 36ee67284c035235, the record's own engine, never read as the engine that stands; class S); the arm is off this page, and its record is kept whole in `docs/history/SCENARIOS_30_2026-10-24.md`.

**Its monitors.** This section draws no canvas: its views are tables and text the page builds from its records.

## B Reading Guide

**The page, in its own words.**

**Monitor Wall Legend**

Each monitor carries its signal and its word (green GAINING, low in its band with room to climb; yellow STEADY, inside its band; red REDLINING, at the top of its band, blinking red and black; red OVER THE LINE, past its band, blinking red and white). The control panel (section 1) holds every switch and knob: the kill switch, the heartbeat (10, 15, 20, 30, 60, 90 or 120 s), the sweep over every beat, the active profile with Restore factory profile and Return to native, the actuation and each route's bill, the mechanism's own knobs, the plant's conditions and faults, the twenty scenarios, and one breaker per monitor. The engine runs itself: its own mathematics decides every beat; the knobs show the mechanism, they never have to drive it.

Five blocks: 1, benefit and organism (twelve monitors, three rows of four on a laptop); 2, real runs on GitHub; 3, brain and mechanism; 4, clouds, chips, orchestrators and scale; 5, proof and telemetry. Each block reads left to right, then the next row. A click on a tile goes to its monitor. The monitor list (appendix E) gives each monitor's source and feed.

The same canvases the sections draw, copied small four times a second; nothing is drawn twice from different data. The page's own supervisory loop (section 5j) leaves a monitor resting while it is off screen, so while this wall is in view the resting monitors are drawn in turn, one a frame, each through its own drawing code; every monitor is drawn once as the page opens, so no tile waits for its section to be scrolled to. Two monitors that sit side by side in their section, native beside Omni-Compass, sit side by side on the wall too: a pair never splits across two rows. Each tile's head is its number and its title in capital letters, then its section and its state (live, replay, from the records, a model, or awaiting its run). A tile with the accent border is a monitor on screen now.

**Organism Ring Guide**

**0 Monitor Wall**

The wall's light count is the one count the breakers, the section signals and the dock print.

Redline Reflex. At the top of its band Omni-Compass pushes at once; where a muscle stays over its line three decisions, stuck, and native's twin reads lower by more than 5%, it hands the muscle to native for 10 decisions (doubling for each hand-back in a row, up to 160), and takes it back only when its side is under the line and lower than native's by 5%; where native is not stronger it stays, because redlining less than native is still a benefit against native. Every light still carries its red: if a muscle redlines on Omni-Compass's side and the reflex cannot stop it (a fault, a bug), it is seen at once, and the kill switch hands everything back to native in the same instant. The rule entered the engine with omni-v21 and stands in the engine that stands, omni-v25, in its immune system, knob by knob (omnicompass/immune.py, the same file in both fingerprints, its C++ twin cpp/src/immune.cpp, proven alike on 200 seeded scripts, and the nervous system's native_hold; on Kubernetes each service's autoscaler target, native's twin the operator's own target), beside the SLO reflex and the pod reflex of Omni-Compass's Kubernetes controller (omni_controller/controller.py); this page's live model (class S) applies the same rule, with the engine's own numbers, to every muscle it draws.

Green, GAINING: low in its band with room to climb, or a GAIN. Dark, AWAITING: the run that fills it has not landed. Grey, NO LINE: a monitor with no line to cross (a bill, a count of runs, a digest check) or no reading yet; neither is ever counted green. Yellow, STEADY: inside its band, or level with native (a change under 0.5%). Red, REDLINING, blinking red and black: at the top of its band and holding there, or a LOSS row. Red, OVER THE LINE, blinking red and white: past the top of its band. Native is orange everywhere, Omni-Compass blue; red is never a side, only the alarm. A monitor that draws two sides carries three lights in its button: native's, Omni-Compass's, each read from its own side on the same load, and the reading between them now by the key (GAIN green, STEADY yellow, LOSS red), so the moment one side turns a colour the other does not is seen as it happens. REDLINING comes before the line: a gauge within 3% of the top of its band, or whose slope over its last readings reaches it within 3 readings. A needle that falls is not a LOSS and one that rises is not a GAIN: each light reads what its gauge is for (a wait that falls is GAINING; work that falls is not). Click a light for its reason; a light turned red by the knobs goes back with the factory tune. The three-reading projection is the light's own. The brain's law acts on the position inside its band and damps on the position's rate of change (K[D] × v, omnicompass/compass_law.py), with a fail-up at the band's 95% wall; it projects nothing ahead. Each light reads the series its monitor draws, and a light fed by our own models says so (class S); hover it for the reading.

**1 Control Panel**

Fuse Box. The first row is the master switches: each acts on the whole page at once, through the same code as the console and the dock, both sides alike (the kill switch hands everything back to native; the dial, the body and the time). Under it, seven tabs: Drive (the plant's conditions and faults, the heartbeat, the profile with its actuation, its contract and each route's bill), Mechanism Knobs (the engine's own constants, one group at a time), Scenarios and Injection Grid, Monitor Breakers (one per monitor: its light and word, green GAINING, yellow STEADY, red REDLINING, red OVER THE LINE; ON runs it live, REST stops its drawing; a way to it), Prometheus Metrics (the live model as a Prometheus endpoint, the same series section 3b exposes), Commands (the request a running Omni-Compass controller would take for each press), Auxiliary Input (ten slots, each waiting to be wired to an organism of your own: its Prometheus, the native it runs, Omni-Compass on top or not; a wired slot lights its own row on the board under the wall, and one press flips the wall's monitors that carry a Prometheus query to it; it is set beside the repository's real records only, never a model run) and All Tabs (the whole panel at once). Every switch is logged with its decision; Back to the factory tune puts all of it back.

Mechanism Knobs. Not the plant's conditions of the Drive tab: these turn the engine's own equations while it runs. Each knob is a constant of the brain (omnicompass/core.py), the compass law (omnicompass/compass_law.py), the adapter's law (omnicompass/adapter.py, nervous_system.py), the redline reflex (omnicompass/immune.py and cpp/src/immune.cpp: the push at the band wall, the streak, the relief share, the native-stronger margin and the hand-back holds), Omni-Compass's push, lift-off and tune-down on each muscle, the model's native controller, or one muscle's body. Turned, the next decision of the live stress-test model is computed with it, on Omni-Compass's side; native's controller knobs move every native controller, the one under Omni-Compass on native too. The harness replays recorded positions, so turning a knob moves the live benchmark engine to the model. A knob turned from the factory turns violet; ↺ puts that one back; Back to the factory tune puts every one back. The monitors (the strip, the rings, the phase plane, the brain's state, the status board) show the mechanism of action as it changes.

The compass law, the push, lift-off and tune-down, native's controller and every muscle's body move the muscles at once. The brain's four pieces move its own state (E, U, I[U], S, B on the brain's monitors) and its grants: at the max every organ may add capacity unless a security hold stands, so the brain's calm acts through the give-backs; at the tune-down the four pieces move the muscles too.

Manuals. The Live Benchmark Engine Manual reads this page top to bottom: how to read, operate, tune and watch it, native beside Omni-Compass, chapter by chapter in the page's own order, and, in its last appendix, the page word for word, every section, every monitor under its number and every knob, written from this page at every build. The Operator's Manual wires and runs Omni-Compass on a stack; the Owner's Manual holds the whole mechanism, its mathematics, its source and every result. Each carries the notice, the LICENSE, the NOTICE and `DISCLOSURES.md`.

**2a Scorecard**

Each row is one way to run a workload, against native alone on the same work.

**2c Benefit Board**

Every benchmark's measured effect in one board, native against Omni-Compass on top of native, by domain and by gauge family, and the Omni index that weighs them together. The whole organism composed from these same tables is in section 5d; every table, every muscle and the grid in full in section 5e.

**2e Status Board**

The tiles, in order: the switch and the engine; the live organism's signals, native beside Omni-Compass, on the four signals of site reliability practice (errors, traffic, latency and saturation), here the muscle-decisions past their line, the work done, and the stand-in each body has (the work waiting in line on the live model, the electrical power drawn on the harness), each named on its tile, with the injected faults beside them; the three-run tables and the verdict guard; GitHub's runs, the clouds and the age of this page. Under the tiles, every alarm and watch item, worst first.

**3a Live Organism**

The system rendered as an organism: each node is a muscle (one controlled actuator) and each ring its operating range. The left instance runs under its native controllers alone; the right runs the same controllers with Omni-Compass's brain, nerves, heart and immune system supervising them.

Every view is run twice on the same body, the same load and the same seed: native (left), then Omni-Compass (right). "One organism" is the sixty muscles in one body: the whole native organism on the left, the same organism with Omni-Compass on the right; "each muscle alone" runs each muscle native and then with Omni-Compass, one at a time. The counters under each ring and the scoreboard (section 3f) give both arms and the difference. Inside one organism the bone, the power and cooling the whole body shares, holds most moves back. A dashed white ring marks every muscle that differs between the two sides at that moment; the counter under each ring counts them.

Every room and every machine pool has a native twin: the same muscle under its own controller, on native's heat and load, the body's memory of healthy. A room that stands warmer than its twin is inflamed. Where its own knob can cool it, it is cured on the spot. Where it cannot, it sends the whole body a signal, its heat debt, like a cytokine calling for help. The other muscles answer: the machine pools give back what Omni-Compass added until the room stands no warmer than native's. The skeleton (the bone: the power and cooling lines the whole body shares) grants nothing it cannot carry. A cooling setting is walked back, never snapped. A pool never loses a machine its queue needs, a battery is timed for its line, and no sense is locked.

Under the max a muscle is moved only where it has room above native. A muscle already at its limit on native ([filled in as the page opens] of these sixty) is left native by design. In these harness views the max acts muscle by muscle and the brain at the center reads the body without steering it, as in the realms harness itself; section 4c shows the live controller, where the brain's rules run every decision.

**3c Control Console**

Runtime parameter control and fault injection on the live benchmark engine: load scaling, noise injection, sensor latency, capacity loss, a demand surge, a sensor blackout, a security hold, the dial, and the kill switch. Every control is applied to both arms identically; the response is plotted per decision, native beside Omni-Compass.

The engine these knobs turn is the engine's own mathematics running in this page: the brain (omnicompass/core.py, integrated by RK4), the compass law (omnicompass/compass_law.py) and the verdict (omnicompass/verdict.py). docs/engine/check_live_engine.py proves the port equal to the Python and the C++ on every build. It supervises the stress-test model's sixty muscles (class S), each on top of its own native controller. Every knob acts on both sides at once, the same demand, the same draw of noise, the same lost pool, so the one difference left between them is Omni-Compass. The knobs move the live benchmark engine here and in sections 3a, 3b, 3e to 3g, 3i and 5h, and the kill switch and the frame load also move this page's own renderer (section 5j). A recorded run (the benefit board, the chips' and Kubernetes' replays, the official runs) is a record: no knob rewrites it.

**3d Heartbeat Dial**

Each beat stands beside native with what it buys (the reads of the cluster a day) and what it costs (Omni-Compass's own processor, and every service row read against native and against the main engine's 60 s).

**3e Control Cycle**

One control cycle traced end to end at reduced speed: the sensed inputs, the brain's decision, and the actuator (muscle) it wrote.

**3f Phase Plane and Scoreboard**

The phase-plane state of every muscle (its position and velocity in its band) for both arms at the same instant, with the running scoreboard.

The plane is a phase portrait, the picture control engineers use for a system's state. Across is where each muscle sits in its band: p is 0 when calm, 0.5 at the center and 1 at its line. Up and down is how fast it moves there (v, per decision). A muscle at rest sits on the middle line. One climbing toward its line moves up and right, and past the right edge it is past its line. Native is on the left and Omni-Compass on the right: the same muscles at the same instant, one dot each. In the realms harness each muscle answers to its own rule, so no single field is drawn. In the stress-test model the shading on the right is the force the compass law would apply there: the add force gives capacity and the hand-back force returns it. Past the 0.95 wall the law fails up at once. Under each plane the same instant is written in words.

**3g Brain State**

The brain's six state variables rendered on their potential-energy landscapes: a state resting at a minimum is stable; a state driven toward a maximum is under load.

The wells draw the same state the cycle panel (section 3e) prints. Each ball is a live value of the engine's brain, integrated by RK4 every decision. In the stress-test model the brain's push and target feed the body's levers. Native has none of this: no state, no memory of need, no stress, no tide; each controller sees only its own reading, one to three decisions late.

**3h Verdict**

Before a knob moves one step past native, the step runs as a trial: costs at native first, then at the step. The engine that stands reads the change exactly as a report reads a row, on the log of each cost, with its 95% interval. The lab opens wired as the shipped Kubernetes controller is wired, on the noise the real runs measured.

**3i Dashboards**

The dashboards draw the same live benchmark engine the ways the industry draws a system: a board of panels, a host map, a graph of one series, an event stream, a cluster console with a button on every workload, a one-line diagram with its breakers, a wall of numbers, the cost of each body part, and Omni-Compass's own form, the two-way pipeline that reads, decides, writes and reads back. On a phone each drawn form also writes what it shows as plain lines under the drawing.

A muscle is any machine, service or controller Omni-Compass can read and set; native is the system as it runs without Omni-Compass; a write is one setting Omni-Compass changed; read back means it checks the setting landed; the lease walks every setting back to native after a crash; the kill switch stops Omni-Compass at once, for security. Every benchmark puts Omni-Compass on top of a native setup and reads both sides by the same key.

**4a Kubernetes Run Playback**

Playback of the official benchmark runs from their archived raw files: the native cluster and the Omni-Compass cluster under identical load, in 30-second bins, every file verified against its run's SHA-256 manifest.

Native (the whole cluster with everything it ships with, everything but Omni-Compass) against Omni-Compass, the same load, the same second, each file's SHA-256 matched before a byte is drawn. The tiles, the candles, the heatmap, the bars and the controller's ticker all move together with section 4b (pick a benchmark, a run or a repetition in either place). Every word is the run's own reading, never one drawn from the stream.

**4b Repetition Telemetry**

Per-repetition telemetry in 30-second bins, with the run's receipt and each repetition's score from the repository's own scorer.

Native and Omni-Compass, repetition by repetition, played in 30-second bins. Every Kubernetes repetition is scored by the repository's own scorer (tools/live_reps.py); where a run holds its own scored summary, the scorer's mean over its repetitions equals it (the count is in section 4b).

**4c Controller Decision Trace**

The shipped controller's decision trace, replayed from its own audit log one decision at a time: its inputs, its choice and its writes.

**4d Cloud Providers**

In a cloud the bill is read beside the work: whether the same bill buys more work with Omni-Compass on top. Each provider's card lists its four test sets and their status.

In a cloud, Omni-Compass shows two things: that the engine holds on the cloud's machines as it does on site, and the work per dollar: more work for the same bill, never a smaller bill as the aim (the founder's words of 31 October). From 31 October every cloud set runs the whole engine at the max on top of the cloud's own autoscaler (the founder's order of that day: no hard rule): the controller senses the service, moves its pod target inside the autoscaler's cover, conveys the processor, and gives back no machine the cloud made (at the max the controller parks none, omni_controller/controller.py; parking is the tune-down's); it asks the cloud for no machine itself, and the cloud's autoscaler alone adds machines. Every gauge is read against native by the one key, and the cloud's judged number is the work per dollar: the bill may rise where the work rises more (the founder's words of 31 October: "anybody will pay 1% more for 10% more product"), a rise shown beside the work it bought, and a tune whose work per dollar falls against native is the tune falling out wrong, fixed in our models. From 31 October, night, every cloud set carries the load whose work grows with the speed (the work meter), so each receipt reads the work per dollar; Azure's queue runs a tuning case first, once, to freeze the counted rates. Until 31 October every cloud set ran at the tune-down on the machines alone (no pod autoscaler moved, no processor conveyed, no power capped), and those sets stand as their own record. Azure, Google Cloud and Amazon run the same tuning, mechanism and harness.

A cloud is the wire between the software end (the owner's programs, Kubernetes and the open-source orchestrators, managers and autoscalers that carry the work) and the hardware end (the bare metal: the GPU chips, the Linux racks, the data center, on-site or off-site). Omni-Compass can be wired in at all three: at the software end, through the cloud's own Kubernetes, and on bare metal, where it also reaches the chip's own knobs through the card's published interface; in each it acts in one place, the software end, on top of the controllers already there. The wire makes no benefit of its own. On the wire alone (every cloud run on this page so far: Omni-Compass at the tune-down on the machines only, the pods exactly native's, the cloud's rule until 31 October), all it could do was park machines the cloud's own autoscaler already made, after its own trial, so the most those runs can show is a small cut in the bill of the wire. From 31 October every cloud set runs the whole engine at the max on the cloud's cluster, so its receipt shows that the engine holds there as on site and how much of the software end's gain comes through the wire, read as work per dollar beside the bill. It cannot show the energy and throughput gained at the bare metal, because the provider's machines are not a tenant's to meter. Omni-Compass on the software end (the front end) and on the bare metal (the back end) reaches gauges a cloud run cannot. A cloud provider that runs Omni-Compass on its own back end would see those gauges on its own GPU chips and Linux racks; no tenant can meter a provider's back end, so this page claims it only as the same mechanism measured on hardware we can meter (section 4f), never as a measurement of the provider's machines. The three places compose into one organism and one bill in section 5d.

No figure before its runs. No place carries a figure before its runs read it (the founder's order of 2 November: no number on any page that no table carries); the readings of each place stand in the place table of sections 4d and 5d.

**4e Orchestrators and Governors**

These products sit between the work and the cluster: batch schedulers and job queues, multi-cluster governors, the default Kubernetes scheduler, GPU and edge orchestrators, and the rightsizing controllers. Each has three runs, and every row is shown.

**4f GPU and CPU Hardware**

The chip is the far end of every stack: there the max would ride each card's own limit, and every watt it takes is power given, shown beside the work it buys.

Each stack is run twice on the same load and the same seed: native first, then Omni-Compass. Left is native, right is Omni-Compass, and every table gives both columns and the difference. The clouds are section 4d and Kubernetes sections 4a to 4c; every muscle of the catalog, the seven organisms and the three-run tables are in section 5e.

**4g CI Pipeline**

The canvas and the table list every GitHub Actions workflow run in progress or queued at build time. The hardware and cloud runs, with their terminal status, are in sections 4d and 4f; every run on the engine that stands that terminated without a measurement is raised on the status board (section 2e).

**5a Python Runs and Monte Carlo**

The Python's runs, the C++ harness's and this page's, run by run: the jump generator's set GitHub ran in the Python (its run linked), the same draws run through the C++ harness on the engine's C++ twins, and the same set recomputed here by this page's own port of the engine as the page opens; every receipt compared three ways, one SHA-256 digest each, and the digest of the draws themselves; and the Python's 500 scenarios a seed replayed from its own record with every trace hash; and the Python's Monte Carlo of the engine's core, 500 runs each from step 0 through the fourth-order Runge–Kutta step, 20 macro steps of 10 micro steps, native beside Omni-Compass, into the basin, held and certified, with every set the jump generator draws here held to the Python's digest.

**5b C++ Twin**

The engine is written twice, in Python and in C++, line for line. Every time this page is built, the Python's own runs are fed into the C++ as its input, and the C++ answer is set beside the Python's, number by number. Where they are the same to the last digit, the two are one engine. Where a part runs in Python only, the list Python-Only Components names it.

**5c Scenarios and Injection Grid**

The ten bodies (the seven organisms: the spine, the four realms, the four stacked and the whole tower; the orchestra, the eighth; this page's own sixty-muscle organism; and the whole organism in its three places), each run two ways: native alone, and Omni-Compass on top of native (the factory tune).

Injection Grid. One batch of the Python's Monte Carlo injections (tools/engine_runs.py: base B, injection k drawing seed B + k·0x9E3779B9 mod 2³²; the C++ engine runs the same seeds to an identical digest, section 5a) runs through every organism the harness runs (the nine bodies of cells 1 to 9: the seven organisms, the orchestra and this page's own sixty-muscle organism, each with a plant; the whole organism is a composition of measured tables and takes no injection), each native and with Omni-Compass on native, and through each of the fourteen governors and orchestrators sitting on the same organism (the third arm): the governor on the organism, and Omni-Compass on top of the governor. Every cell is the one batch cut down: its 500 runs, of which 100 runs are the first 100, 10 the first 10 and 1 the first; and in each run a stack of 500 organisms, of which 100 are the first 100 copies, 10 the first 10 and 1 the first (copy c of run k on injection k + c, mod 500). A click on 100 shows those 100 and no others; a cell whose runs × organisms pass 500 uses an injection more than once: the organism axis is a resampling of the same 500 injections and adds no independent evidence, so the cells at 500 organisms read as the cells at 1 do. A cell's word is one batch's (one run in the key's sense), its injections within that batch, never "our runs". Each run's change against its reference is read over its 24 blocks of ten decisions, and the cell's word by the key over its N runs (tools/readings.py three_run_reading, the 0.5% floor). The page re-hashes the record's cells and recomputes every cell's mean from the runs' means; both checks are printed under the batch.

Governors in the Injection Grid. In our models each governor is the body of the catalog muscles that stand for its knobs (tools/run_orchestra.py, the mapping of tools/governors_page.py), run on the organism's clock on the same injections, each under its own native controllers; Omni-Compass, supervisory on top of that product, steers both bodies. The product's body does not reach the organism's own muscles, so its way reads its own muscles beside the organism's.

**5d Whole Organism**

Omni-Compass is wired into every piece of a stack through its own two-way plug: the software (the cluster: Kubernetes, its add-ons, its governors and orchestrators; and the services on it: the pools, the consumers, the caches), the bare metal under it (the chip's power limit and clock; the machine's kernel and its own meter) and, where the stack is rented, the cloud that bills the machines. A stack is bought one of two ways: on-site bare metal, the software on the owner's own bare metal (software × bare metal, no cloud); through the cloud, the cloud's build, its bare metal at the back end and the software (cloud × bare metal × software), with the cloud as two pieces beside it (cloud × software, the back-end bare metal left to the provider).

Every piece's benefit is added into one collective reading, gauge by gauge (work, the 95th percentile response, machines, energy, the bill), with the receipts under it: what native spent, what Omni-Compass spent, and the benefit, in the units each table measured. The repository composes the layers in one place, tools/whole_system.py, which writes `results/WHOLE_SYSTEM.md` from the tables that stand: each layer measured against its own native with the layer under it as native runs it; a gauge's ratio oriented so that above 1 is a GAIN at every layer alike; the layers composed by the product law, R(g) = ∏ℓ Rℓ(g), log R = Σℓ log Rℓ (for small benefits the product is their sum), every interval carried (the exact Student t per run; A, B and C pooled by inverse variance; the layers' variances added); two readings of the same numbers (every measured piece: everything measured enters with its interval; the strict reading, the index's rule: only what held the same way in all three of our runs, everything else exactly 1); and the separability assumption tested on the governors' four ways. Section 5d recomputes nothing from the tables: it regroups that file's layers into the two ways (docs/engine/export_whole.py), summing the logarithms of the layers a scenario stacks and adding their variances, as the file's rule composes any set of layers; the one number is the mean of the gauge logarithms, its 95% interval under the file's two bounds.

**5e Measured Effect**

Everything the board in section 2c stands on, from the organisms down to the single muscle: the seven organisms under their three loads, all 1,107 muscles of the catalog one cell each, every registered benchmark still to run, the scale-out grid of copies and runs, and every three-run table in the repository with every row as it is written.

That board holds every benchmark's benefit in one place, as the repository's benefit sheet holds it (results/BENEFIT_SHEET.json), with the Omni index that weighs them together (`results/OMNI_INDEX.md`). The scale-out grid holds every organism at 1, 10, 100 and 1,000 copies, each at 1, 10, 100 and 1,000 runs, modelled and with the real cluster inside.

Seven Organisms Under Three Loads. The seven organisms of the catalog (the spine first, the base every stack runs on; the four realms; the four stacked; the whole tower), each run native and with Omni-Compass on top of native, under its own load, the climb (1 2 3 2 3 4 …) and work waiting in line; and the whole organism, the software, the cloud and the hardware stacked into one body (section 5d), composed from the pieces' own readings, native and with Omni-Compass on top of every piece: a composition with two arms, not an organism of the catalog. The orchestra, every governor's muscles as one body, is the eighth organism (section 4e). The monitor draws the seven from the realms table; the whole stack's two arms are section 5d's composition until the stacked run, preregistered in `docs/WHOLE_SYSTEM_PREREGISTRATION.md`, measures them in one run.

Open Register Slots. Every muscle in them (a legged robot's gait, locomotion_speed; a wind turbine's blade pitch, pitch_angle_limit; a mobile robot's travel speed, agv_speed and trajectory_speed) already runs, modelled, in the 1,107-muscle catalog and the seven organisms of section 5e, native and with Omni-Compass on top. A slot here is the next step for that kind of muscle: the same test on the outside world's own software (MuJoCo's Unitree Go2, NREL's OpenFAST, ROS 2 Nav2), each queued until its three runs land.

The 1,000 tier runs on GitHub's free runners only; paid machines stay at 100. Each bar and square carries its own word with its size and no sign; the raw change keeps its own sign, labelled raw.

**5f Telemetry Analytics**

The same telemetry analysed four ways: a three-dimensional tensor view, a difference matrix, a principal-component decomposition and Omni-Compass's own account of its run; the service level and error budget in SRE terms; and Omni-Compass answering the viewer's questions about itself from the page's own records.

The tensor fills in decision by decision as section 3a plays, and the same numbers are read three more ways: the difference between the arms, the body's principal motions, and Omni-Compass's own account of what it is doing. The official runs (class L) are read as an SRE reads a service: the service level, the error budget burned and every gauge against native; and Omni-Compass answers questions about itself, reading the page's own records through tools and turning the dashboard to a run.

**5g Master Switch Wiring**

One switch for the whole organism, drawn as the repository wires it: the master switch holds the brain's outputs; the brain sits above the organism; every muscle hangs under it with native's own controller under every muscle, never switched. Every Omni-Compass loop of this page is listed with its state at the switch.

**5h Specification and Mathematics**

The component specification: every part, its equations and the source module that implements it, as the repository writes them.

**5i Failure Modes and Controls**

The failure-mode register: every known way Omni-Compass can fall short, the control that mitigates it, that control's coverage, and the residual risk where it does not reach. The second table: the resource costs the three-run tables report but do not judge.

**5j Self-Governed Rendering**

The page's own render loop is governed by the same compass law, measured on the client device, native beside Omni-Compass.

Each chart is a muscle whose knob is how many frames it waits between draws, and the reading is the time between frames. Below the band's center the pedal lifts and every chart is handed back to native, drawn every frame. Toward the line it pushes: the charts farthest from where you are looking wait a frame or more, so the frame itself stays inside its line. Every step is a trial run to its full measurement and kept only when the frame shortens; a step that buys nothing is refused and every chart handed back to native (the reflex rule: where a knob cannot bring a GAIN, it stays native).

## C Problem and Answer

**The page, in its own words.**

**Problem, Answer, Proof, Limits and Protection**

**Industry Problem**

Demand Outrunning Capacity. Demand for computing is growing faster than the power, the machines and the budgets built to carry it. The International Energy Agency puts the world's data centres at about 415 TWh of electricity in 2024, around 1.5% of all the electricity the world used, and expects that to more than double, to about 945 TWh, by 2030, with artificial intelligence the largest driver. In the United States, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory counts 176 TWh in 2023, 4.4% of the country's electricity, and between 6.7% and 12% by 2028. The usual answer is to build more: more machines, more megawatts, more cooling and a larger bill.

Idle Capacity. Across the Kubernetes clusters Cast AI measured in 2025, the average processor was 8% busy and the average memory 20% used, read before its own automation was switched on. The organizations Flexera surveyed estimate that 29% of what they spend on cloud infrastructure and platforms is wasted.

Coordination Gap. In every realm (compute, AI and the cloud; physics, robotics and autonomy; energy, facilities and industry; distribution and specialized systems) each machine already runs under local feedback loops: horizontal autoscalers, firmware power and clock governors, building-management loops, battery-management systems, connection pools, servo controllers. Each loop is tuned on its own, against its own setpoint, with margins sized for a worst case. Four costs follow, and every operator pays them:

Reaction Latency. Reactive loops act on lagging, averaged signals. A Kubernetes Horizontal Pod Autoscaler recomputes its replicas every 15 seconds by default and damps its scale-downs over a 5-minute stabilization window; new pods then need time to schedule and start. Queues form before capacity arrives.

Static Over-Provisioning. Worst-case margins persist through the far more common nominal regime. The operating point sits well inside the safe region, and the headroom between it and the constraint boundary is paid for and rarely used.

Uncoordinated Coupling. Loops share resources: power, cooling, connections, memory, network. One loop's correction is another loop's disturbance: more web replicas saturate the database's connections; more cooling pushes a power feed toward its limit.

Gaps Closed by Hand. Operators close them with fixed settings, runbooks, alerts and on-call rotations, and the settings go stale as the load moves. That is safe, and it leaves the region between the operating point and the safe boundary unused nearly all of the time.

**The Industry's Three-Layer Attack, and Where Omni-Compass Sits**

The cost of computing is attacked in three layers at once: the chip, the cloud and the software. Each layer's controllers are inner loops: each closes its own loop, on its own signal, at its own clock. Omni-Compass is a supervisory controller in a cascade on top of them: the native controllers stay in charge of their own actuation, and Omni-Compass, the outer loop, reads the whole body and moves the setpoints they already accept, in the control plane only (one wire in to read a controller's meters, one wire out to write its setting; no byte of the workload passes through it). Its actuators reach all three layers through each layer's own interface: on the chip the card's power limit and clock ceiling under the operator's cap; in the cloud, where every set runs the whole engine at the max on top of the cloud's own autoscaler since 31 October, the machines that autoscaler made: one more woken at once when a reading crosses its line, none given back below the count the cluster ran on its own, the autoscaler alone adding or removing machines; in the software the autoscalers' targets, replicas ahead of the load, the processor a machine can spare, a database's pool, a cache's memory, a broker's consumers.

No product of anyone else was benchmarked here: the tables say what layer each class of tool acts in, never that one falls short, and nothing here claims Omni-Compass is the only approach, a smaller bill, or fewer people. A resource Omni-Compass spends is shown beside the work it bought as power given, so no "no trade-off" claim is made. The full page: `docs/INDUSTRY_LANDSCAPE.md`; the products of each layer, each from its own public material and graded on seven measures: `docs/LANDSCAPE.md`.

**Omni-Compass Answer**

Omni-Compass is a supervisory controller. It sits on top of every native controller and never in place of one; native keeps running underneath, always. It treats each setting a native controller already exposes (a replica floor, a power cap, a clock ceiling, a temperature setpoint, a pool size) as a muscle, and the whole stack as one body, the organism. Through a two-way plug, one wire in (the readings native already reports) and one wire out (the settings native already exposes), it reads every muscle, places each reading in the muscle's own band, from calm to its line (the limit it must not cross: a response time, a temperature, a feed's rating), and moves each setting one bounded, smooth step at a time. By default every muscle is driven up to its own limit, never past it and never below native; every write is read back; at the max, the factory setting, nothing is given back below native, so no give-back trial runs; at the tune-down (the dial's save setting) every give-back below native is tried in its own paired trial (the muscle with and without it, under the same load) and undone if the muscle loses by it, by the verdict's own rule: refused and undone where the 95% interval of its change in cost lies wholly above zero, or where the median or the mean of its logarithmic cost rises past the 2% allowance (omnicompass/verdict.py); a raise is held inside the muscle's own bounds and judged afterwards by the three-run tables; and wherever no gain can be brought, the muscle stays native. One brain steers the whole body: one shared calm, read from the brain's state over the whole organism, gates every give-back in every organ (an organ gives capacity back only while that calm stands at or above its own threshold, its service line is clean and its sense is fresh; omnicompass/nervous_system.py), while every raise stays inside the muscle's own band. Every muscle is judged on itself; a trade between muscles, one used less so another carries more, is not in the engine that stands. The aim: more work, faster, on fewer machines, with less energy. Where Omni-Compass takes more energy to buy more work or speed, that energy is shown beside what it bought, as power given, and never called a loss.

**Costs and Responses**

| Industry cost | Omni-Compass response | Judged by |
|---|---|---|
| Reaction Latency: waiting, queues and time over the line before capacity arrives | it reads every muscle on its own clock (on Kubernetes its full read of the state every 10 seconds on site and every 60 seconds through a cloud at the factory, any beat from 10 to 120 seconds where a person picks it on the dial, and its fast checks every 5 seconds) and moves as a reading nears its line, not after the line is crossed: proactive as well as reactive, like an immune system | time waited, missed or failed: a fall is GAIN, a rise is LOSS |
| Static Over-Provisioning: machines, energy and a bill paid for headroom rarely used | every muscle driven up to its own limit and never past it, so the machines already paid for carry more work; in a public cloud it runs the whole engine at the max on top of the cloud's own autoscaler (every set since 31 October; the sets at the tune-down on the machines alone, under the order of 14 October, are their record): it never gives back a machine below the count the cluster ran on its own, wakes one more at once when a reading crosses its line and asks for pods ahead of the load, so the cloud's autoscaler may add machines for them; the machines it takes are power given, read beside the work per dollar; the bills measured so far are single runs (read from the cloud ledger as the page opens), and no three-run bill exists yet | work done: a rise is GAIN; machines, energy and the bill: a fall is a cut, a GAIN, and a rise is power given |
| Uncoordinated Coupling: one loop's correction is another loop's disturbance | one brain for one body: one shared calm over the whole organism gates every give-back in every organ, and every raise stays inside the muscle's own band; a trade between muscles is not in the engine that stands | every muscle judged on itself, and the organism judged whole |
| Gaps Closed by Hand: settings retuned by hand, alert minutes, on-call | an autopilot: every write read back, every give-back below native (at the tune-down) on its own trial, every reason written to its audit; an alert minute on Omni-Compass's side is found and fixed, never accepted | the alert minutes, judged; the settings commands it wrote on its own, shown |

Section Map. Reaction latency: the organism live, every muscle's place in its band and the decisions past the line, native beside Omni-Compass (3a), and the shipped controller's decision trace (4c). Static over-provisioning: the benefit board (2c) and the clouds' bills (4d). Uncoordinated coupling: the whole organism (5d) and the organism's immune system (3a). The gaps closed by hand: the status board's alarms (2e) and the golden signals, the event log and the live metrics (3b).

Limits. It replaces no controller, and it creates no capacity a machine does not have: it uses the capacity already there, each muscle to its own limit. It promises no number on a system it has not run on: the only number that applies to a system is the one that system's own paired runs produce. The outside figures above are their authors' own, quoted for scale; none was measured, checked or endorsed by Dubra Software Inc. / The Omni-Compass LLC, and none is a result of Omni-Compass.

Accountability. Every comparison judged pairs two arms, native and Omni-Compass on top of native, on the same machines with the same work (the governors' tests run four arms and pair them two at a time, section 4e). Every three-run table is run three separate times (A, B and C); the Azure runs read so far and the card's run inside the whole organism's composition are single runs, named as such where they appear. Every reading is one word, GAIN, STEADY or LOSS, decided by what the gauge is for (the key, appendix D); every result carries its evidence class (P a physical meter, L live software, S our own models, never shown as proof of hardware); and every row is shown, LOSS included. The rule over all of it is do no harm, in its one meaning (`DISCLOSURES.md`, section 3): the aim is an organism never left turning out less than native, nor less healthy; where a row of a three-run table reads LOSS against native (section 2d lists each, every one; a model's own LOSS rows, class S, stand in their own sections, the heartbeat dial's in 3d), the row stays, its cause is to be found and fixed (the LOSS ledger writes a cause beside those it has; the others read "not yet written"), and the test runs again on the engine that stands. The executive summary (appendix D) reads where the record stands, from the records on this page.

Outside Sources. International Energy Agency, Energy and AI (April 2025), its executive summary. Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory, 2024 United States Data Center Energy Usage Report (December 2024), prepared for the U.S. Department of Energy. Cast AI, 2026 State of Kubernetes Optimization Report: a seller of Kubernetes optimization, reporting its own customers' clusters as they ran before its automation. Flexera, 2026 State of the Cloud Report (March 2026): a survey of 753 cloud decision-makers and users, the figure their own estimate. The Kubernetes documentation, Horizontal Pod Autoscaling: the controller's defaults.

## G Disclosures, Law and Contact

**The page, in its own words.**

THIS PAGE IS A LIVE TUNING ENGINE. THE BENCHMARKS ARE ONE TUNED ENGINE. Every knob, button and monitor on this page can be turned in real time: the heartbeat at 10, 15, 20, 30, 60, 90 or 120 s, the dial between the max and the tune-down, the pedal, each muscle driven or left native, up or down, in or out, minus or plus, to see and produce the variations of the mechanism of action with native alone, with Omni-Compass on top of native, or both side by side. Like any engine, where turning the mixture, the intake, the exhaust, the firing or the pistons changes what it delivers, turning any knob can change every metric, either way, in any direction a tuner chooses: that range is the engine's versatility, one engine for many vessels, tuned many ways, for many outputs. What the page shows at any setting other than the factory tune demonstrates that range and is never a benchmark result. Every benchmark result is one engine at its one factory tune: the engine its table names, every benchmark run again on the engine that stands (section 4g). From omni-v17 the factory tune is chosen by a declared rule: of the tunes that harm no gauge against native, the one against which no other tried tune reads GAIN on one service gauge and LOSS on none, searched on development seeds and read again on untouched ones (`docs/THE_TUNE.md`; `DISCLOSURES.md`, section 3). A tune other than the factory tune is the tuner's own, and its results are its own. The factory profile is locked as the main engine: the page opens on it, every link opens on it, and only a person pressing a knob moves it off. A turned knob never breaks the engine; it changes its mechanism of action, how its muscles and the organism share the work, toward the purpose the new setting asks for. Reading every knob against the factory profile, one at a time, is the standing work of the sweeps, and a profile they find that reads GAIN against the factory profile on a judged gauge, with no LOSS on any, lands as the next engine version (`docs/FACTORY_PROFILE.md`).

DISCLOSURE: REAL RUNS, A REAL ENGINE, NOTHING HARDWIRED. Every run played back on this page (sections 4a and 4b) is read from the raw files of benchmark runs executed on GitHub's own machines, each file checked against its run's SHA-256 list before a single number is drawn; every three-run table is read as the repository holds it, each naming its runs, and the raw branch holds their folders as they are archived (the status board, section 2e, marks each run whose raw files are archived). Every model view runs the engine's own code (the compass law, the brain, the verdict) live in this page, linked through software to the repository's Python and C++ and checked against them on every build. GitHub rebuilds the page from the repository whenever the engine, a model or a result changes, and every three hours: no number on it is typed in by hand, and when the repository changes, the page changes with it.

## Every knob

| Knob | What it does | Where it is |
|---|---|---|
| Kill Switch | Omni-Compass ON (green) or engaged (red): every setting handed back to native at once; every Omni-Compass side follows native (white) | the kill switch (section 1; its wiring, section 5g) |
| Tune Dial | the max (every muscle to its own limit) or the tune-down (never more than native) | the console's dial |
| Demand | ×0.25 to ×2 of the day's own demand, both arms alike | the console's demand slider |
| Noise | σ of the demand's noise, one draw a muscle a decision, both arms alike | the console's noise slider |
| Sense Delay | decisions added between what a muscle does and what its controller reads, both arms alike | the console's delay slider |
| Faults | a surge, half the machines lost, a sense blind, a security hold, on the chosen body part | the console's fault buttons |
| Speed | 1×, 4×, 16× or 60× of the engine's clock | the console's time knob |
| One Decision | step the engine one decision while paused | the console's time knob |
| Pause | hold the engine's clock | the console's time knob |
| Frame Load | +0, +8 or +16 ms burned inside every frame of this page | the console's frame-load knob |
| Run | A, B or C (or the archived run) the monitor plays | the monitor's own buttons |
| Repetition | which repetition of the run is replayed | the monitor's own buttons |
| Arm | which arm stands on the right: Omni-Compass governing, or watching only | the monitor's own buttons |
| Stack | the GPU card, the Linux host, Kubernetes or the cloud | the monitor's own buttons |
| Governor | which orchestrator or scheduler is shown | the monitor's own table |
| Scenario | the software end carried to bare metal, or to the cloud | the monitor's own buttons |
| Reading | every measured piece carried, or the index's strict rule | the monitor's own buttons |
| Chip Run | the chip's one run beside the count or in it | the monitor's own buttons |
| Beat | the governor's full read of the state and its decision: every 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 60, 75, 90 or 120 s in the heartbeat sweep's record; on a real governor its --interval (the factory's beat is the governor's own default: 15 s on omni-v16, 60 s from omni-v17 to omni-v23, and from omni-v24 the factory beat for its place, 10 s on site and 60 s through a cloud) | the monitor's own buttons |
| Engine | which engine's record the monitor reads: the engine that stands or the one before it | the monitor's own buttons |
| Baseline | what each reading is set against: native, or the same engine at another setting | the monitor's own buttons |
| Gauge | which gauge of the run is shown | the monitor's own buttons |
| Filter | which rows, words, families or organisms are shown | the monitor's own buttons |
| Body | which body the organism runs: the harness, the organism, every muscle alone | the organism's own buttons |
| Muscle Switch | pick one muscle on either ring: its card, and its own switch, which hands that one muscle back to native (Omni-Compass OFF on it) or puts Omni-Compass on it again; the kill switch hands every muscle back at once | the organism's monitors and the muscle's card (section 3a) |
| Runs | how many of the Python's runs this page computes again | the monitor's own buttons |
| Print | the receipts of the runs, printable, with the digests and the notice | the monitor's own button |
| Seed Base | which held-out seed base's 500 scenarios are replayed | the monitor's own buttons |
| Set | the Python's own 500 (numpy, seed 223387268) or a set the jump generator drew on this page | the Monte Carlo monitor's set knob (section 5a) |
| Omni-Compass Way | the bounded command toward the nearest basin, toward the far basin, or with the double well off (μ = 0); native is the plant alone (u = 0) | the Monte Carlo monitor's way knob (section 5a) |
| Play / Pause | the cursor's sweep of the 20 macro steps, held or running | the Monte Carlo monitor (section 5a) |
| Custom System | your stack written as ranges of the core's six states and 22 parameters, run in the page or on GitHub through the Python and the C++ (`systems/README.md`) | the Monte Carlo monitor's own-system panel (section 5a) |
